### WebAuthn Platform-Coverage Pass (2026-09-01)

A dedicated pass ran 2026-09-01 to settle the §Open Questions row this spec had carried since 2026-04 — whether any Electron WebAuthn module covers Windows and Linux — and to fix the per-platform strategy §WebAuthn Platform-Authenticator Native Module now states. Windows header claims were verified by fetching `webauthn.h` itself rather than a summary; Electron's own surface was verified against the release-branch documentation rather than `main`, because `main` documents options the V1 runtime does not carry. Four claims that entered the pass as hypotheses were **falsified and are recorded as such**, on the selection rule that each would have produced a wrong gate — a fifth falsified expectation, that Yubico discussion #731 fixed a `libfido2` version floor, was a mis-citation rather than a gate and is recorded on its own row instead: the two Windows PRF fields are not both on the assertion-options struct; struct sub-versions do not track API versions; the raw `hmac-secret` extension identifier is registration-only; and the Windows Hello PRF build floor has no Microsoft primary source. A feasibility spike ran on the same date against the real runtime — Electron 44.1.1 / Chromium 152.0.7977.65 / Node 24.19.0 (ABI 149) on macOS 26.6.1 arm64 — and its readings are carried as **Measured (ours)** rows, so grades across this table are Primary source / Source / Documentation / Package / Measured (ours), and a claim is graded by what the cited artifact or reading actually establishes rather than by the reputation of its host.

| Source | Type | Key Finding | URL |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `electron-webauthn` npm manifest | Package | The successor to the deprecated `@electron-webauthn/native` describes itself as a "Cross-platform loader for native WebAuthn support **on macOS**"; its sole optional dependency is `@electron-webauthn/macos`, which declares `"os": ["darwin"]`. There is no Windows or Linux implementation package. | <https://www.npmjs.com/package/electron-webauthn> |
| `electron-webauthn` repository README | Source | States the mechanism in the author's own words: the root package "is a cross-platform shim that lazy-loads `@electron-webauthn/macos` only on macOS. This keeps Linux/Windows packaging safe for apps that include but do not use WebAuthn outside macOS." PRF and largeBlob are platform-authenticator-only; Electron 28+, macOS 12.0+. | <https://github.com/iamEvanYT/electron-webauthn> |
| Electron `app.configureWebAuthn` (release-branch docs) | Documentation | Electron **does** ship a native macOS platform authenticator: `app.configureWebAuthn({ touchID: { keychainAccessGroup, promptReason? } })`, tagged `_macOS_`. Credentials live in the macOS keychain "bound to this device's Secure Enclave" — device-bound, not iCloud-synced. Until it is called, `PublicKeyCredential.isUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable()` resolves `false`. The API documentation states no PRF behavior. | <https://www.electronjs.org/docs/latest/api/app> |
| electron/electron #51255 | Primary source | "feat: support WebAuthn Touch ID platform authenticator on macOS" — merged to `main` 2026-04-30, the change that makes this spec's prior "Electron provides no platform-authenticator prompts on macOS, Windows, or Linux" sentence false for macOS. | <https://github.com/electron/electron/pull/51255> |
| electron/electron #51411 | Primary source | The same feature backported to the release branches (merged into `42-x-y` 2026-05-01); the API is present in the `44-x-y` branch documentation, so it ships in the V1 runtime. The `platformPasskeys` option documented on `main` is **not** on the 44 branch. | <https://github.com/electron/electron/pull/51411> |
| electron/electron #24573 | Primary source | Open, last active 2026-08-27: public-key credentials are refused on a custom-scheme origin, which is what the renderer's `sidekicks-renderer://` scheme is — the reason the ceremony cannot move into the renderer even where a platform authenticator now exists. The issue thread frames the refusal as an https requirement; the Measured (ours) row below and the Chromium source row show the check is an **origin allowlist** rather than a secure-context test, which is a stronger claim in the same direction — scheme privilege does not lift it. | <https://github.com/electron/electron/issues/24573> |
| microsoft/webauthn `webauthn.h` | Primary source | `WEBAUTHN_API_VERSION_9` is current. `WebAuthNAuthenticatorMakeCredential(HWND, …)`, `WebAuthNAuthenticatorGetAssertion(HWND, LPCWSTR pwszRpId, …)`, `WebAuthNIsUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable(BOOL*)`, and `WebAuthNGetApiVersionNumber()` are all declared in the v1 baseline block. `bEnablePrf` and `pPRFGlobalEval` are on **MakeCredential** options (struct v6 / v8); only `pHmacSecretSaltValues` is on GetAssertion options (struct v6). Struct sub-versions do **not** track API versions: that assertion struct ships in the `WEBAUTHN_API_VERSION_4` delta block, so assertion-time PRF gates on API ≥ 4, not ≥ 6. The raw `hmac-secret` identifier is registration-only ("GetAssertion Input Type: Not Supported"). Windows applies `SHA-256(UTF8Encode("WebAuthn PRF") ‖ 0x00 ‖ Value)` unless `WEBAUTHN_AUTHENTICATOR_HMAC_SECRET_VALUES_FLAG` (`0x00100000`) is set and raw 32-byte salts are supplied — and that flag is a `dwFlags` bit on **both** options structs, though the header says so only in `pPRFGlobalEval`'s comment. Cancellation is a two-call pair: `WebAuthNGetCancellationId(_Out_ GUID*)` and `WebAuthNCancelCurrentOperation(_In_ const GUID*)`, with `pCancellationId` on MakeCredential options at struct v2 and on GetAssertion options at struct v3; **both APIs and both members are v1 baseline**, so cancellation is gated on no API version. Two traps: `bPrfEnabled` is a different identifier from `bEnablePrf` — an _output_ on `WEBAUTHN_CREDENTIAL_ATTESTATION` v5, not the MakeCredential input; and MakeCredential's `dwFlags` still carries a stale `Reserved for future Use` comment that contradicts `pPRFGlobalEval`'s instruction to set a flag in it. Re-verified 2026-09-01 against commit `0ef86c2f` (2025-09-11), 1381 lines. | <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/microsoft/webauthn/master/webauthn.h> |
| Microsoft Learn — `WebAuthNAuthenticatorMakeCredential` | Documentation | The API is ordinary documented Win32 (`api_type: DllExport`, `api_location: WebAuthn.dll`) with **no** entitlement, capability, package identity, or browser restriction stated; `Remarks` says the caller is "typically" a relying-party client application. `hWnd` is "the handle of the window used to display the WebAuthn UI"; the operation is modal and is cancelled through `WebAuthNCancelCurrentOperation`, never by abandoning the call — and that call takes the GUID from `WebAuthNGetCancellationId`, which must have been placed on the request's own `pCancellationId` member, so a binding that omits the getter has no way to cancel at all. The requirements table publishes **no** minimum Windows version, which is itself an argument for gating on `WebAuthNGetApiVersionNumber()` rather than on an OS build. | <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/webauthn/nf-webauthn-webauthnauthenticatormakecredential> |
| go-ctap/winhello | Primary source | Independent non-browser consumer of `webauthn.dll` — Go, cgo-free plain syscalls, Apache-2.0, claiming `hmac-secret` / `prf` extension support. Empirical proof that a non-browser process may drive this API. | <https://github.com/go-ctap/winhello> |
| Microsoft Learn — `WebAuthNIsUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable` | Documentation | Scopes the call to the **platform** authenticator: it "determines whether a user-verifying platform authenticator is available on this device", and "a platform authenticator is one built into the client device, such as Windows Hello". Nothing in it reports on roaming authenticators, and it takes no transport or attachment parameter, so a `FALSE` is not evidence that an attached security key is unusable — cross-platform authenticators are selected on the unrelated `dwAuthenticatorAttachment` / `WEBAUTHN_CTAP_TRANSPORT_*` axes. A negative is also not an error: the call returns `S_OK` and writes `FALSE`. Cited to Learn rather than to the header because `webauthn.h` carries **no** comment on this prototype at all — checked, not assumed. | <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/webauthn/nf-webauthn-webauthnisuserverifyingplatformauthenticatoravailable> |
| MichaelGrafnetter/webauthn-interop | Primary source | Second independent non-browser consumer — a managed .NET wrapper over the same `webauthn.h` surface, MIT, shipping a NuGet interop package and a PowerShell module used against Entra ID and Okta. Its README does not mention PRF, so PRF support **there** is unconfirmed; the citation carries the not-browser-restricted claim only. | <https://github.com/MichaelGrafnetter/webauthn-interop> |
| KB5077181 release notes | Primary source | Confirms the build / KB / date pairing the community PRF-floor claim rests on — KB5077181, 2026-02-10, OS builds 26200.7840 (25H2) and 26100.7840 (24H2) — and is **silent on the claim itself**: the notes contain none of "WebAuthn", "PRF", "hmac-secret", "passkey", "Windows Hello", or "FIDO". | <https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/topic/february-10-2026-kb5077181-os-builds-26200-7840-and-26100-7840-f0fa9e54-a22a-4a06-96b6-bf5b2aded506> |
| Corbado — passkeys and WebAuthn PRF | Source | The origin of the Windows Hello PRF build-floor claim: Windows Hello gained PRF in the February 2026 cumulative update, patching `hmac-secret` into 25H2 build 26200.7840+, with earlier 25H2 builds unsupported. Vendor blog — competent and commercially interested; **graded Source, and no gate encodes its build number**. | <https://www.corbado.com/blog/passkeys-prf-webauthn> |
| bitwarden/clients #19858 | Source | Independent field corroboration of the same floor from a different party — Windows Hello reported PRF-capable on 25H2 builds 26200.8117 and 26200.8039. Cites no Microsoft source, which is why the floor stays `Source (corroborated)`. | <https://github.com/bitwarden/clients/issues/19858> |
| Yubico/libfido2 discussion #731 | Primary source | Not a version floor (the pass's hypothesis was wrong about this citation) but a live API-usage trap: `webauthn.dll` silently drops the `hmac-secret` request at credential creation unless a **discoverable / resident** credential is also requested — confirmed on Win11 22H2 at API version 4 by USB traffic capture. A registration path that wants PRF and omits the resident-key requirement gets a credential that cannot do PRF later, with no error. | <https://github.com/Yubico/libfido2/discussions/731> |
| microsoft/webauthn `webauthn.h` — version-history block | Primary source | Read at the last commit that modified the header (`0ef86c2f`, 2025-09-11, "Update WebAuthn API Version to WEBAUTHN_API_VERSION_9"; blob `b5eaca30`). Establishes the API-version → struct-version mapping this spec now tabulates, and one negative that decides its shape: the header carries **no** `added in WEBAUTHN_API_VERSION_N` comment inside any struct body — every in-struct delta names the struct's own version constant, and API-to-struct appears only in the top-of-file block (lines 36–154), each entry a delta from its predecessor. `pHmacSecretSaltValues` sits under `GET_ASSERTION_OPTIONS_VERSION_6` and is listed in the `WEBAUTHN_API_VERSION_4` block, confirming the API-4 floor; there is no `cbHmacSecretSaltValues` member, the count being `WEBAUTHN_HMAC_SECRET_SALT_VALUES.cCredWithHmacSecretSaltList`. | <https://github.com/microsoft/webauthn/blob/master/webauthn.h> |
| Yubico `libfido2` — `fido_cred_new(3)` / `fido_cred_set_authdata(3)` | Primary source | The credential read-back contract. "The authenticator data returned by `fido_cred_authdata_ptr` is a CBOR-encoded byte string, as obtained from the authenticator. To obtain the decoded byte string, use `fido_cred_authdata_raw_ptr`." `fido_cred_x5c_ptr` is the leaf certificate only, the chain requiring `fido_cred_x5c_list_count` / `_list_ptr` / `_list_len`. Returned pointers are "guaranteed to exist until any API function that takes `cred` without the `const` qualifier is invoked". There is **no** getter for a whole attestation object — `fido_cred_set_attobj` is a setter only, documented as useful "in applications interfacing with the WebAuthn API". | <https://developers.yubico.com/libfido2/Manuals/fido_cred_new.html> |
| Yubico `libfido2` — `fido_assert_new(3)` / `fido_assert_set_authdata(3)` | Primary source | The assertion read-back contract, and the reason every accessor takes an index: "A FIDO2 assertion is a collection of statements". `fido_assert_authdata_ptr` is likewise CBOR-wrapped with `_raw_ptr` the decoded form; `fido_assert_id_ptr` is the credential ID and `fido_assert_user_id_ptr` the user handle. `fido_*_set_clientdata` takes the **unhashed** client data and hashes internally — "For compatibility with Windows Hello, applications should use `fido_assert_set_clientdata` instead of `fido_assert_set_clientdata_hash`" — and no getter for the unhashed bytes exists. | <https://developers.yubico.com/libfido2/Manuals/fido_assert_new.html> |
| Yubico `libfido2` — `fido_dev_open(3)` / `fido_dev_get_assert(3)` / `fido_dev_set_io_functions(3)` | Primary source | Why Linux cancellation is not the Windows shape. `fido_dev_get_assert` "is synchronous and will block if necessary". `fido_dev_cancel` has no man page of its own — it is a link into `FIDO_DEV_OPEN(3)`, where its entire documentation is "The `fido_dev_cancel` function cancels any pending requests on `dev`", and it is omitted from that page's own `RETURN VALUES` section despite returning `int`. A grep of every `man/` page for thread-safety language finds two hits, both in `fido_init(3)` and neither about device handles; the README has none. The documented interruption mechanisms are `fido_dev_set_timeout` ("not to block for more than `ms` milliseconds"; a timeout fails with `FIDO_ERR_RX`) and `fido_dev_set_sigmask`. | <https://developers.yubico.com/libfido2/Manuals/fido_dev_open.html> |
| `koffi` — asynchronous calls | Documentation | The async contract both FFI legs take: "You can issue asynchronous calls by calling the function through its async member… with `(err, res)` parameters"; "These calls are executed by worker threads. It is your responsibility to deal with data sharing issues"; "You can easily convert this callback-style async function to a promise-based version with `util.promisify()`"; "Variadic functions cannot be called asynchronously." A queued-call ceiling (`max_async_calls`, default 256) throws when exceeded. Separately, `koffi.alloc` is the documented way to obtain a pointer that "won't move" for memory the native side may write "at a later time, maybe during a later call or from another thread" — buffers and typed arrays are explicitly "not safe" there. | <https://koffi.dev/load#asynchronous-calls> |
| `@simplewebauthn/server` — npm manifest, repository, and JSR metadata | Package | The verification dependency Plan-016 T6.2 adopts, read 2026-09-01: `13.3.3` published 2026-08-26, **MIT**, `engines.node >= 20.0.0`. Crypto is obtained solely through `globalThis.crypto` (`getWebCrypto.ts`), with no Node built-in specifier anywhere in the shipped ESM or CJS build or across its transitive dependency tree — which is what makes the JSR-declared `runtimeCompat` `workerd: true` credible for the control plane. That support is **declared and not CI-tested**: the project's own `ciChecks.yml` runs a Node 20/22 × Deno matrix and no `workerd` job, which is why T6.2 carries a deployment-target smoke test. `userVerified` is exposed on both verification results and `newCounter` on the authentication result. The project states it is not open to external contributions. | <https://www.npmjs.com/package/@simplewebauthn/server> |
| Yubico `libfido2` — `NEWS`, `LICENSE`, `README.adoc` | Primary source | BSD-2-Clause. Current release 1.17.0, tagged 2026-04-15 (PGP-signed by a Yubico maintainer). The repository publishes **no** GitHub Releases at all, so version discovery goes through tags, `NEWS`, or the distro package. Vendor install line confirms the Debian binary package `libfido2-1`, hence `libfido2.so.1` as the load target. | <https://github.com/Yubico/libfido2> |
| libfido2 `fido_assert_set_extensions` / `fido_cred_set_extensions` man pages | Documentation | `FIDO_EXT_HMAC_SECRET` is supported on both the assertion and credential sides, with `FIDO_EXT_HMAC_SECRET_MC` additionally on the credential side (the CTAP 2.2 registration-time evaluation, the analog of Windows `pPRFGlobalEval`). The assertion page also records that the derived secret "varies according to whether user verification was performed by the authenticator" — a UV and a non-UV assertion derive different secrets. | <https://developers.yubico.com/libfido2/Manuals/fido_assert_set_extensions.html> |
| libfido2 enumeration + CBOR-info man pages | Documentation | `fido_dev_info_new` / `fido_dev_info_manifest` / `fido_dev_info_ptr` / `fido_dev_open` enumerate and open devices, but enumeration alone does **not** establish `hmac-secret` capability: that needs `fido_dev_get_cbor_info` plus a scan of `fido_cbor_info_extensions_ptr` for the literal `"hmac-secret"`. Yubico's own PRF deep-dive states the same probe. | <https://developers.yubico.com/libfido2/Manuals/fido_cbor_info_new.html> |
| FIDO device access on Linux — `systemd` ≥ 244, not a rules package | Primary source | **Corrected 2026-09-01 at Codex round 1; the round-1 text of this row was wrong and is restated rather than quietly dropped.** It said the `uaccess` rules "ship with the distro package" (`libfido2`). They do not, and no supported release ships `70-u2f.rules` at all. Upstream libfido2 does carry `udev/70-u2f.rules` — a 101-entry VID/PID allowlist — but installing it is opt-in on `UDEV_RULES_DIR`, which Debian, Ubuntu, and Fedora do not set; verified against the file lists, `libfido2-1` contains only the shared object and two doc files on Debian sid / trixie and Ubuntu noble, and `fido2-tools` and `libfido2-dev` carry no rules either. `libu2f-udev` has been an **empty transitional package** since Debian bookworm (Debian #951201, changelog `1.1.10-2`, 2021-01-14) and is absent from sid; Fedora's `u2f-hidraw-policy` is **retired**, its `dead.package` reading "Obsoleted by systemd", with `systemd.spec` carrying the `Obsoletes:` pair. Access instead comes from systemd's **descriptor-based autodetection**, added in **244** (PR #13357, commit `d45ee2f3`; `60-fido-id.rules` present at tag `v244`, absent at `v243`) and requiring **two** files in sequence: `60-fido-id.rules` (package `udev`) runs the `fido_id` helper on `hidraw` and sets `ID_SECURITY_TOKEN=1`, and `70-uaccess.rules` (package `systemd`) turns that into `TAG+="uaccess"`. Neither alone suffices. | <https://github.com/systemd/systemd/pull/13357> |
| `uaccess` is a seat ACL, not a permission bit | Primary source | The load-bearing limit on the row above, and the reason the leg's diagnostic cannot say "you are not in the right group". `uaccess` grants a POSIX ACL to the user holding an **active session on the device's seat** (`sd-login(3)`: logind manages these ACLs "as long as the user has an active session on it"). `/dev/hidraw*` takes no `MODE` or `GROUP` default from udev, so it stays `0600 root:root` and the ACL is the only grant — which means a headless daemon, a system service, an SSH session, or a container gets **nothing**, and switching seats revokes it from the previously-active user. The retired `70-u2f.rules` also set `GROUP="plugdev", MODE="0660"`, which did work headless; systemd's replacement deliberately does not. "Modern systemd removes the need for a rules package" is therefore true for **interactive desktop sessions only**, which is exactly the shape this app ships in. | <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/systemd/systemd/main/rules.d/70-uaccess.rules.in> |
| libfido2 — the make-credential chain and `fido_opt_t` | Primary source | The registration side the round-1 text left unspecified, verified at 1.17.0 (2026-04-15). `fido_cred_new` → `fido_cred_set_type` → `fido_cred_set_clientdata_hash` → `fido_cred_set_rp` → `fido_cred_set_user` → **`fido_cred_set_extensions(fido_cred_t *, int flags)`** with **`FIDO_EXT_HMAC_SECRET`** (`0x01`, an OR-able bitmask, not an enum) → `fido_cred_set_rk` → `fido_cred_set_uv` → `fido_dev_make_cred(dev, cred, pin)`, reading back through `fido_cred_id_ptr` / `_len`. `fido_cred_set_rk` and `fido_cred_set_uv` take `fido_opt_t` = `FIDO_OPT_OMIT` (default, authenticator decides) / `FIDO_OPT_FALSE` / `FIDO_OPT_TRUE`. Two traps: the legacy `fido_cred_set_options(cred, bool, bool)` is still exported from `fido.h` but absent from the man page's `NAME` list and must not be used; and `FIDO_EXT_HMAC_SECRET_MC` (`0x20`) is a **different** CTAP 2.2 extension returning output at make-credential time, not the one this leg wants. The assertion-side mask `FIDO_EXT_ASSERT_MASK` is narrower than the credential-side one. | <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Yubico/libfido2/main/man/fido_cred_set_authdata.3> |
| CTAP 2.1 §12.5 — hmac-secret output depends on UV | Primary source | Why the assertion's UV mode must be pinned rather than left to the authenticator. The authenticator mints **two** independent 32-byte per-credential secrets at registration, `CredRandomWithUV` and `CredRandomWithoutUV`, and at assertion "chooses which CredRandom to use … based on whether user verification was done" — selected by the **`uv` bit actually set in the response**, not by what the platform requested. The same salt therefore yields two unrelated keys. libfido2 says the same in its own man pages ("the resulting hmac-secret varies according to whether user verification was performed") and defaults both `fido_assert_set_uv` and `fido_cred_set_uv` to `FIDO_OPT_OMIT`, letting the authenticator decide — so a leg that does not pin UV has a **non-deterministic** key derivation. | <https://fidoalliance.org/specs/fido-v2.1-ps-20210615/fido-client-to-authenticator-protocol-v2.1-ps-20210615.html#sctn-hmac-secret-extension> |
| `fido_dev_info_manifest` cannot report a permission failure | Primary source | The call "always returns `FIDO_OK`"; on a discovery error it sets `*olen` to 0. Worse for diagnostics, a key the process cannot open is **enumerated and then silently dropped**: `hid_linux.c`'s `is_fido()` opens the node `O_RDWR`, and on `EACCES` falls through with `usage_page == 0`, so `copy_info()` discards the entry without incrementing `*olen`. "No key attached" and "key attached, not accessible" are therefore **indistinguishable through libfido2** — the `EACCES` reaches only free-text debug logging. The distinguishing check must be built outside the library. | <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Yubico/libfido2/main/man/fido_dev_info_manifest.3> |
| libfido2 transport scope — `README.adoc` and `src/dev.c` | Primary source | Added 2026-09-01 (Codex round 2), correcting a **BLE** claim this spec's per-platform table carried. The library's own scope sentence is "communicate with a FIDO device over USB or NFC" — BLE appears nowhere — and `fido_dev_info_manifest`'s body runs the `hid` backend unconditionally and the `nfc`, `pcsc`, and `winhello` backends under `USE_NFC` / `USE_PCSC` / `USE_WINHELLO`. In `CMakeLists.txt` the Linux NFC option `NFC_LINUX` defaults **ON** (forced `OFF` off Linux) and the experimental `USE_PCSC` defaults **ON** as well, so a default Linux build is HID + NFC; because this leg never bundles the library, the host build decides. No flag in that file adds a BLE backend, so a BLE-only key has no path through this binding at all. | <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Yubico/libfido2/main/src/dev.c> |
| Apple — `LAPolicy.deviceOwnerAuthentication` vs `…WithBiometrics` | Documentation | Added 2026-09-01 (Codex round 2), the source of the probe correction. `deviceOwnerAuthenticationWithBiometrics` is "user authentication with biometry" and evaluates false when biometry is unavailable or unenrolled and system-wide after too many consecutive failures; `deviceOwnerAuthentication` is "user authentication with biometry, Apple Watch, or the device passcode", and on macOS resolves to Touch ID, a nearby paired Apple Watch running watchOS 6+, or the user's password (macOS 10.11+). Neither is a statement about platform-authenticator availability, which is why the macOS probe now gates nothing. | <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/localauthentication/lapolicy/deviceownerauthentication> |
| Xcode 26.4 release notes | Documentation | The macOS addon's toolchain floor, set by the SDK and not by the language: Xcode 26.4 includes the macOS **26.4** SDK — the one that carries the security-key `prf` property — bundles **Swift 6.3**, and itself requires a Mac running macOS Tahoe **26.2** or later. Added 2026-09-01 (Codex round 3). | <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/xcode-release-notes/xcode-26_4-release-notes> |
| Apple — `ASAuthorizationPublicKeyCredentialAssertion` | Documentation | The macOS side of guard 4's response check: the protocol declares exactly three properties — `signature`, `userID`, and **`rawAuthenticatorData`** — so the returned UV flag is read from that byte sequence, there being no typed accessor for it. Added 2026-09-01 (Codex round 3). | <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/authenticationservices/asauthorizationpublickeycredentialassertion> |
| Debian and Fedora `libfido2` package indexes | Package | `libfido2-1` / `libfido2-dev` / `fido2-tools` in Debian back to bullseye (trixie 1.15.0, sid 1.17.0-2) across amd64, arm64, armhf and more; Fedora ships `libfido2` + `fido2-tools` (F45 / Rawhide 1.17.0). The Linux leg therefore depends on a library the target distributions already package. | <https://packages.debian.org/search?keywords=libfido2&searchon=names&suite=all&section=all> |
| Chromium `device/fido/` directory census | Primary source | Platform-authenticator backends exist per OS as `mac/`, `win/`, and `cros/`; there is **no** `linux/`. `hid/` and `cable/` are roaming transports, not platform authenticators. An absence in a census bounds availability from below rather than above, which is why the Linux finding is carried with the three corroborating sources below rather than on this alone. | <https://api.github.com/repos/chromium/chromium/contents/device/fido> |
| passkeys.dev device-support matrix | Documentation | The FIDO Alliance / W3C community matrix lists Ubuntu "Native Platform APIs" as **Planned**; synced passkeys, autofill UI, and passkey upgrades are served on Linux only by browser extensions. | <https://passkeys.dev/device-support/> |
| FOSDEM 2026 — Credentials for Linux | Source | The maintainers of the project building the replacement, stating the position as of 2026-02: "the Linux desktop still has no standard FIDO2 platform APIs for browsers and native apps," with TPM-backed platform authenticators named as **future** work. | <https://fosdem.org/2026/schedule/event/838A8N-credentials-for-linux-bringing-passkeys-to-linux/> |
| linux-credentials/credentialsd | Primary source | The in-flight D-Bus service and proposed XDG credential portal (LGPL-3.0, partly SUSE-sponsored, active into July 2026) whose landing with a TPM backend is this spec's named re-evaluation trigger for the Linux leg. Its sibling `libwebauthn` is the Rust FIDO2 library underneath. | <https://github.com/linux-credentials/credentialsd> |
| Chromium — WebAuthn origin restrictions | Primary source | The source-of-truth statement of what Chromium will accept as a WebAuthn origin: https, `localhost`, and extension origins. It is an **allowlist**, not the secure-context predicate — which is why a custom scheme registered `secure: true` is a secure context and still refused. | <https://chromium.googlesource.com/chromium/src/+/main/content/browser/webauth/origins.md> |
| Custom-scheme renderer under Electron 44.1.1 | Measured (ours) | From a renderer on `sidekicks-renderer://` registered `standard: true, secure: true`: `isSecureContext === true` and `PublicKeyCredential` is defined, yet `navigator.credentials.create()` rejects `SecurityError: Public-key credentials are only available to HTTPS origins with valid certificates, HTTP origins that fall under 'localhost', or pages served from an extension.` Two reads **do** answer there and cost no credential — `PublicKeyCredential.getClientCapabilities()` (returning `extension:prf: true`, `userVerifyingPlatformAuthenticator: false` on an unsigned build) and `isUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable()`. | — |
| `app.configureWebAuthn` on Electron 44.1.1 | Measured (ours) | Present (`typeof === 'function'`), returns `undefined`, and is honoured: on an unsigned build the main process logs `FIDO: touch_id_context.mm:89 Touch ID authenticator unavailable because keychain-access-group entitlement is missing or incorrect.` It drives only the renderer's `navigator.credentials`, which the origin allowlist has already closed, so it is not the V1 path. | — |
| `electron-webauthn-mac` 1.0.0 shipped package | Measured (ours) | Prebuilt N-API addon (`native/webauthn_mac_addon.node`, 398,690 B unpacked, Swift + `AuthenticationServices`, no `node-gyp` and no Xcode CLT at install); loads in the Electron 44 main process in 219 ms / 576 KiB RSS. **Two disqualifiers**: it generates the challenge internally (32 random bytes — the caller cannot supply the relying party's, per its own README "Differences from Browser WebAuthn"), and the binary is arm64-only and non-fat (`lipo -info`) with no `os` / `cpu` field and no arch guard, so a `darwin-x64` install succeeds and fails at `require`. Also unsupported: `rp.name`, `timeout`, `pubKeyCredParams` (ES256 only). Its unsigned failure is a plain `Error` reading `The calling process does not have an application identifier. Make sure it is properly configured.` | <https://www.npmjs.com/package/electron-webauthn-mac> |
| Apple — `ASAuthorizationPlatformPublicKeyCredentialProvider` | Documentation | The macOS 12.0+ provider the own-built addon uses. Its `createCredentialAssertionRequest(challenge: Data)` and `createCredentialRegistrationRequest(challenge: Data, name: String, userID: Data)` both take the **caller's** challenge, which is the property the reference implementation lacks. | <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/authenticationservices/asauthorizationplatformpublickeycredentialprovider> |
| Apple — `ASAuthorizationSecurityKeyPublicKeyCredentialProvider` | Documentation | The macOS **12.0+** roaming-key provider the addon's second arm uses, with `createCredentialAssertionRequest(challenge: Data)` and `createCredentialRegistrationRequest(challenge:displayName:name:userID:)` — one argument wider than the platform provider's registration call. Its requests carry PRF through a `prf` property added only at macOS **26.4** (verified on both the Swift and Obj-C pages, `beta: false`), and the framework declares **no** `extensions` member on any request class — enumerated across the framework index rather than inferred, since Apple models WebAuthn extensions as individually-named properties. | <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/authenticationservices/asauthorizationsecuritykeypublickeycredentialprovider> |
| Apple — security-key `prf` property | Documentation | `var prf: ASAuthorizationPublicKeyCredentialPRFAssertionInput? { get set }`, macOS **26.4**+ — the fact that splits the macOS leg into two PRF floors. On macOS 15.0–26.3 an attached security key can complete sign-in and cannot produce PRF, so that host takes the §Fallback Behavior custody leg rather than a refusal. A single "PRF on macOS 15.0+" floor would be wrong for roaming keys. | <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/authenticationservices/asauthorizationsecuritykeypublickeycredentialassertionrequest/prf-7pp6b> |
| W3C WebAuthn Level 3 — the `prf` extension | Primary source | The normative home of the extension this whole strategy rests on: §10.1.4 _Pseudo-random function extension (prf)_, anchor `#prf-extension`, in the W3C **Recommendation** of 2026-08-25 — the same document status and date as the `getClientCapabilities()` row below, verified on the same pass. It is cited for where the extension's semantics are defined, including the client-side transform this section's guard 3 applies by hand on Linux; the transform's byte-level form (`SHA-256("WebAuthn PRF" \|\| 0x00 \|\| value)`) is carried in guard 3 from the earlier per-surface pass and is not re-derived here. | <https://www.w3.org/TR/webauthn-3/#prf-extension> |
| Yubico — Developers Guide to PRF | Documentation | The per-credential-salt recommendation this design **records and declines**: "For increased security, a unique, per-credential random salt should be generated by the server during registration and stored alongside the credential ID." It presumes an `allowCredentials` flow in which the server already knows which credential will sign; this deployment's credentials are discoverable (guard 2), so at options time there is no credential to look a salt up for, and serving every candidate's salt instead would publish the user's enrolled-credential set on an unauthenticated reply. Guard 3 states the fixed-constant disposition and what replaces the key separation the recommendation was buying. | <https://developers.yubico.com/WebAuthn/Concepts/PRF_Extension/Developers_Guide_to_PRF.html> |
| W3C WebAuthn Level 3 — `getClientCapabilities()` | Primary source | Defined as a **static member of the Web IDL `PublicKeyCredential` interface** (`static Promise<PublicKeyCredentialClientCapabilities> getClientCapabilities()`), W3C Recommendation 2026-08-25. It is a browser API: the current Node API corpus contains zero occurrences of `PublicKeyCredential`, `navigator.credentials`, or `CredentialsContainer`, so the main process cannot call it and the macOS probe is specified from native calls instead. | <https://www.w3.org/TR/webauthn-3/#sctn-getClientCapabilities> |
| Apple — `LAContext.canEvaluatePolicy(_:error:)` / `ASAuthorizationWebBrowserPublicKeyCredentialManager` | Documentation | The macOS probe's two candidates, and why the weaker one wins. `isDeviceConfiguredForPasskeys` answers the capability question directly but is macOS **26.2+** and sits on a browser-class manager behind the Apple-approval entitlement `com.apple.developer.web-browser.public-key-credential`, which this app does not hold. `canEvaluatePolicy` is macOS 10.10+ and answers whether a device-owner policy can be evaluated — a **proxy**, recorded as one: its equivalence to platform-authenticator availability is nowhere documented, which is why it gates **nothing** and is read as a diagnostic only (re-derived 2026-09-01, Codex round 2). The two policies differ in a way that decides that: `deviceOwnerAuthenticationWithBiometrics` is "user authentication with biometry" and is documented false with no enrolled or available biometry and system-wide after too many failed attempts, while `deviceOwnerAuthentication` is "user authentication with biometry, Apple Watch, or the device passcode" — on macOS, Touch ID, a nearby paired Apple Watch, or the user's password (macOS 10.11+). | <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/localauthentication/lacontext/canevaluatepolicy(_:error:)> |
| Apple — `ASAuthorizationPublicKeyCredentialPRFAssertionInput` | Documentation | The PRF assertion input, macOS **15.0+** (iOS 18.0), with `ASAuthorizationPublicKeyCredentialPRFAssertionInputValues` and `ASAuthorizationPublicKeyCredentialPRFAssertionOutput` on the same floor — so the macOS PRF leg is a macOS 15 capability and the probe, not the OS name, decides it. | <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/authenticationservices/asauthorizationpublickeycredentialprfassertioninput-c.class> |
| `ffi-napi` npm manifest | Package | The alternative FFI, rejected on maintenance rather than capability: `latest` is `4.0.3`, published **2021-03-18**, against `koffi`'s five-year-younger and actively-released line. (The package `node-ffi-napi` does not exist on the registry — that name is the GitHub organization.) | <https://registry.npmjs.org/ffi-napi> |
| `koffi` npm manifest and package contents | Package | MIT. `3.1.6` is `latest` (published 2026-08-20) of 279 versions. The main tarball carries **zero** `.node` binaries; since `3.0.0` the platform binary is one of fifteen `os` / `cpu`-gated `optionalDependencies` (`@koromix/koffi-<platform>-<arch>`), all pinned to the same version, with no install scripts and no dependencies of their own. `win32-arm64` was **absent in `3.0.0`–`3.1.0`** and restored at `3.1.1`, which is the six-platform floor. Verified under pnpm 10 with build scripts blocked: `require('koffi')` and a live FFI call both succeed, so **no `onlyBuiltDependencies` entry is warranted**. | <https://registry.npmjs.org/koffi> |
| `koffi` documentation and loader | Documentation | Declares `napi: 8` and refuses a runtime below it, so an Electron bump needs no rebuild. Documents that "some runtimes (such as Electron) forbid the use of external buffers" — `koffi.view()` throws there, so out-structs must be copied with `koffi.decode()`. Binding happens at module evaluation, so deferral is by guarded dynamic `import()` rather than by any lazy-load feature. | <https://koffi.dev/> |
| `koffi` 3.1.6 under Electron 44.1.1 | Measured (ours) | Warm load in the main process 7.8 ms / 1.70 MiB RSS (cold Node first load 186 ms / 5.05 MiB). Eighteen `webauthn.dll` struct declarations layout-verified and six prototypes parsed; the full `libfido2` binding executed end-to-end in-process — 21 symbols resolved through `fido_init`, the `fido_dev_info_*` enumeration, `fido_assert_set_rp` / `set_clientdata_hash` / `allow_cred` / `set_hmac_salt` / `set_extensions(FIDO_EXT_HMAC_SECRET)` / `set_up` / `set_uv`, and `fido_assert_hmac_secret_ptr` — every setter `FIDO_OK`, with `fido_dev_info_manifest` returning `FIDO_OK` and zero devices, the expected negative on a host with no key attached. | — |
| `webauthn.dll` struct layouts under koffi | Measured (ours) | Pinned constants for the binding's layout tests: `sizeof` MakeCredential options 200, GetAssertion options 200, credential attestation 192, assertion 168, hmac-secret salt 32; `offsetof` GetAssertion `pHmacSecretSaltValues` 104, MakeCredential `bEnablePrf` 96 and `pPRFGlobalEval` 128, assertion `pHmacSecret` 112. Call hazards the binding must honour: returns are signed `HRESULT` read as `int32_t` (`S_OK == 0`); every `PCWSTR` is UTF-16; the `HWND` is the **value** inside `BaseWindow.getNativeWindowHandle()` (`readBigUInt64LE(0)`), never the buffer's address; out-structs are OS-allocated and released with `WebAuthNFreeCredentialAttestation` / `WebAuthNFreeAssertion` after the PRF bytes are copied out; `__stdcall` matters only on ia32, which V1 does not ship. | — |
| Hosted CI capability boundary | Measured (ours) | Runners can verify install and load under Electron on all three OSes, the unsigned-macOS error text, the entitlement diagnostic, the custom-scheme `SecurityError` and both capability reads, `WebAuthNGetApiVersionNumber()` and `WebAuthNIsUserVerifyingPlatformAuthenticatorAvailable()`, every FFI layout and prototype against pinned constants, and the `libfido2` chain with zero devices. They **cannot** produce a PRF byte on any platform — no Touch ID, Hello, TPM, or key — which is why end-to-end PRF is a recorded manual matrix rather than a CI gate. | — |

### Console Surface Sources

The sources behind `§Console Design (Meridian)`'s surface set, its pane and window model, and the daemon-side library rows in §Console Libraries.

- Electron `dialog` API reference — `showOpenDialog`'s `openFile`, `openDirectory` and `multiSelections` properties, and the platform note that one picker cannot offer both files and folders on Windows or Linux; read 2026-09-14. Behind the composer's attach control and its files-only, several-at-once, never-a-folder rule.
- Two Chrome-debug clients on one page, measured 2026-09-14 on Electron 44.1.0, Chromium 152, `playwright-core` 1.62.1, macOS arm64: both clients attach in either order without error, the counter arithmetic is exact with no lost or doubled input, each observes the other's mutations, and the in-process debugger emits no detach event, so neither client needs exclusive ownership of the page. A never-shown window screencasts at about 48 frames a second with zero acknowledgement errors while an external client drives the same page, with background throttling off and painting when initially hidden on. The acknowledgement method must be called by its exact name, or the queue stalls after exactly three frames, which is the frames-in-flight default. A `BrowserWindow`'s never-navigated page target blocks an external attach forever (thirty-second timeout), where a `BaseWindow` completes the same attach in 36 to 37 milliseconds with four processes rather than five. A capture on a never-shown window returns a genuinely doubled image on a display whose scale factor is two while the platform reports a single scale factor, so the scale is read from the returned image. One hidden page view costs about 247 MB of working set and three extra processes over a bare main process, and driving both clients with a screencast adds about 104 MB; on macOS at this Electron the process metrics carry only the working-set figures, so the working set is the figure read.
- Shared emulation state, same measurement: a connected external automation client unconditionally pins focus emulation and a light colour scheme for as long as it is attached, so a screencast running underneath visibly changes theme and focus behaviour. Emulation state, device metrics and network interception are therefore shared mutable state the daemon owns and re-asserts after any client detaches; evaluation, input and the screencast need no such ownership.
- Development-server discovery has no library: every candidate a registry search surfaces checks whether a port is _available_ rather than enumerating listening sockets with their owning process identities, so the daemon does it itself — three platform branches over the machine's listening sockets plus a short-timeout loopback probe, one probe loop per daemon, ticking only while a project session is live.
- Registry facts read 2026-09-21 for the libraries §Console Libraries admits on the daemon side and for the renderer's mark strokes: `@playwright/mcp` 0.0.80 and `playwright-core` 1.62.1 (Apache-2.0; 86 kB and 13.4 MB unpacked), `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` 1.30.0 (MIT, 4.3 MB), `perfect-freehand` 1.2.3 (MIT, 112 kB, no runtime dependencies), `@dagrejs/dagre` 3.1.1 (MIT, 1.4 MB), `croner` 10.0.1 (MIT, 155 kB, no dependencies, Node ≥ 18), `picomatch` 4.0.7 (MIT, 94 kB), and `mermaid` 11.17.2 (MIT, 84.0 MB unpacked and loaded lazily; 12.0.0 published 2026-09-10 is a separate, evidenced bump).
